Duterte rejected the corruption allegations and emphasized that the supplies at the time of purchase were in high demand, hence, the high prices.[^cite_note-351] A Senate probe, headed by [Senate Blue Ribbon Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Blue_Ribbon_Committee) chairman [Richard Gordon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Gordon_\(politician\)), was initiated in August 2021 for alleged irregularities in the use of COVID-19 response funds amounting to ₱67 billion.[^cite_note-352] A month before Duterte left office, Gordon released a draft report on the probe, which claimed that Duterte "betrayed public trust" for appointing Yang as economic adviser in 2018, prohibited his Cabinet and other officials from attending the hearings, and allegedly discredited the Senate and [Commission on Audit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_on_Audit_\(Philippines\)).[^cite_note-353] The report, however, failed to reach the Senate [plenary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plenary_session) for deliberation after it was signed by nine senators out of the 11 required signatures.[^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355]

#### Energy and climate

The Duterte administration initially adopted a "technology neutral" policy in energy generation.[^cite_note-CNBC-EnergyBoom-356] Earlier in his term, Duterte stressed that [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal) remains the most viable source of energy if the Philippines is to accelerate industrialization,[^cite_note-357] and questioned the [sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctions_\(law\)) imposed by the United States and European Union on smaller countries including the Philippines when the country's [carbon footprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_footprint) is not significant compared to the superpowers.[^cite_note-358]

The administration shifted its energy policy to prefer [renewable sources of energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_resource) later in Duterte's term.[^cite_note-PNAGovPH-ShiftToRenewable-359] At his fourth [State of the Nation address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_the_Nation_Address_\(Philippines\)) in July 2019, Duterte issued an order to cut coal dependence and hasten a transition to [renewable energy](25784.md).[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] In October 2020, the [energy department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Energy_\(Philippines\)) issued a [moratorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moratorium_\(law\)) on the construction of new [coal power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal-fired_power_station) and favored renewable energy sources.[^cite_note-362] On February 28, 2022, Duterte issued an executive order approving the inclusion of [nuclear power](22153.md) in the country's energy mix.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364]

To hasten the expansion of the nation's power capacity, Duterte established the inter-agency Energy Investment Coordinating Council tasked with simplifying and streamlining the approval process of big-ticket projects.[^cite_note-CNBC-EnergyBoom-356] On January 21, 2022, he signed a law promoting the use of [microgrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microgrid) systems in unserved and underserved areas to accelerate total electrification of the country.[^cite_note-365] The administration made initiatives to liberalize the energy sector,[^cite_note-PNAGovPH-ShiftToRenewable-359][^cite_note-366] allowing 100% foreign ownership in large-scale [geothermal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_power) projects starting October 2020.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]

Duterte signed the [Paris Agreement on Climate Change](30242372.md) in March 2017, after initially having misgivings about the deal which he says might limit the country's industrialization.[^cite_note-369] The Agreement was ratified by the [Senate](299521.md) on March 15, 2017.[^cite_note-370] Duterte said that rich countries producing the most [carbon emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_emission) must pay smaller countries for damage caused by [climate change](5042951.md).[^cite_note-371]

#### Government streamlining



Duterte signs the [Freedom of Information executive order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Information_Order_\(Philippines\)) in [Davao City](489401.md) on July 24, 2016.

Duterte introduced reforms to eliminate [red tape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_tape) in the government,[^cite_note-372] and ordered government agencies to remove all processes which are "redundant or burdensome" to the public.[^cite_note-373] Three weeks after assuming office, he issued his second executive order establishing [Freedom of Information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Information_Order_\(Philippines\)), allowing citizens to obtain documents and records from public offices under the [executive branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_branch) to promote transparency in the government.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-PNAGovPH-FOISuccessRate-375]

In May 2018, Duterte signed the Ease of Doing Business Act which aims to create a better business environment by reducing processing time, cutting bureaucratic red tape, and eliminating corrupt practices in all government agencies.[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-PIAGovPH-RA11032-377] In December 2020, he enacted a law authorizing the president to expedite the processing and issuance of national and local permits, licenses, and certifications, by suspending its requirements, in times of [national emergency](298608.md).[^cite_note-378]

Duterte institutionalized the [8888 Citizens' Complaint Hotline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8888_\(Philippines\)) in October 2016, allowing the public to report complaints on poor government front-line services and corrupt practices in all government agencies.[^cite_note-379]

#### Health care

Duterte vowed to improve the [health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_care_in_the_Philippines) system,[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] certifying the Universal Healthcare Bill as an urgent measure as early as July 2018.[^cite_note-382] In February 2019, he signed the Universal Health Care Act, which automatically enrolls all Filipinos under the [government's health insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PhilHealth) program.[^cite_note-383] He also enacted the National Integrated Cancer Control Act which establishes a "national integrated" program to control and prevent [cancer](105219.md) by making treatment more accessible and affordable,[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] and the Philippine Mental Health Law, which provides free [mental health services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mental_health_care_in_the_Philippines) down to the [barangay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barangay) level while requiring hospitals to provide psychiatric, psychosocial and neurologic services.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387]

In December 2019, Duterte signed a law institutionalizing [Malasakit Centers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malasakit_Center) in all hospitals run by the [Department of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Health_\(Philippines\)), allowing indigent patients to efficiently access financial medical assistance from various government agencies.[^cite_note-388]

Duterte ordered the full implementation of the [Reproductive Health Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responsible_Parenthood_and_Reproductive_Health_Act_of_2012),[^cite_note-389] [banned smoking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationwide_smoking_ban_order_\(Philippines\)) in public places nationwide,[^cite_note-390] and set a [price cap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_cap) on select medicines.[^cite_note-391]

#### Education

Duterte signed the [Universal Access to Quality Tertiary Education Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Access_to_Quality_Tertiary_Education_Act) in August 2017, providing free college education in all [state universities and colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_universities_and_colleges) nationwide.[^cite_note-392] He signed a law establishing [transnational higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transnational_education) in the country, allowing foreign universities to offer degree programs in the Philippines in an effort to bring international quality standards and expertise into the country.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] He also signed medical [scholarships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scholarship) for deserving students in state universities and colleges or partner private higher education institutions through the Doktor Para sa Bayan Act on December 23, 2020.[^cite_note-395]

Duterte approved in January 2021 a law institutionalizing the [alternative learning system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_Learning_System_\(Philippines\)) (ALS), providing free education to those out of school.[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] In March 2022, he enacted a law granting inclusive education for learners with [disabilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disability).[^cite_note-398]

On June 9, 2020, Duterte signed a law establishing the country's first [National Academy of Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Academy_of_Sports_\(Philippines\)) in [New Clark City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Clark_City), Capas, Tarlac.[^cite_note-399]

### Foreign policy

See also: [List of international presidential trips made by Rodrigo Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Rodrigo_Duterte)



Duterte with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) in [Moscow](19004.md), May 2017



Duterte with Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) in [Pasay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasay), November 2017

The Duterte administration has vowed to pursue what it describes as an "independent foreign policy" that would reject any meddling by foreign governments, reiterating [Article II](19554336.md#Article_2._Declaration_of_Principles_and_State_Policies), Section 7 of the 1987 Constitution which states: "The State shall pursue an independent foreign policy. In its relations with other states the paramount consideration shall be national [sovereignty](28791.md), [territorial integrity](31335.md), [national interest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_interest), and the right to [self-determination](29269.md)." At a September 2016 press conference, Duterte stated: "We will observe and must insist on the time-honored principle of sovereignty, sovereign equality, non-interference and the commitment of peaceful settlements of dispute that will serve our people and protect the interests of our country."[^cite_note-independent_foreign_policy2-33]

Duterte made his first international trips as president to [Vientiane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vientiane), Laos and [Jakarta](16275.md), Indonesia on September 5–9, 2016.[^cite_note-400]

Duterte pursued improved relations with [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China-Philippines_relations) and [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines-Russia_relations),[^cite_note-Storey-2017-401] and lessened the country's dependence on its traditional ally – the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines-United_States_relations).[^cite_note-Diplomat-ForeignPolicy-402][^cite_note-Hendler-2018-403] He has adopted a cautious, pragmatic, and conciliatory stance towards China compared to his predecessor,[^cite_note-404] and has set aside the [previous administration's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benigno_Aquino_III_administration) confrontational policy of asserting the Philippines' [claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spratly_Islands_dispute) over the [South China Sea](74209.md) and its islands.[^cite_note-405]

Militant groups decry the ties between President Duterte and China over the Chinese occupation of contested waters and the reported harassment of the fishermen amidst the [territorial disputes](32005536.md) in the [South China Sea](74209.md).[^cite_note-chinaduterte42-406][^cite_note-407] Also, while the United States is one of the countries critical to Philippine drug war campaign,[^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] most of the militant groups[^cite_note-411] – particularly left-wing groups[^cite_note-MilleniumPost-412] – also criticize Philippine-US relations due to the United States' "[imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_imperialism)"[^cite_note-MilleniumPost-412][^cite_note-413] and [neoliberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberalism) policies.[^cite_note-414]

### Administration and cabinet

Main article: [List of administration members in the presidency of Rodrigo Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_administration_members_in_the_presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte)

This paragraph is an excerpt from [Presidency of Rodrigo Duterte § Administration and cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte#Administration_and_cabinet).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte\&action=edit)]

On May 31, 2016, a few weeks before his presidential inauguration, Duterte named his [Cabinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_the_Philippines),[^cite_note-Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte_Inquirer-DuterteNamesCabinet-415] which consisted of former military generals, childhood friends, classmates, and [leftist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leftist) politicians.[^cite_note-416] Following his presidential inauguration, he administered a mass oath-taking for his Cabinet officials, and held his first Cabinet meeting on June 30.[^cite_note-Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte_PhilStar-CabinetTakesOath-417][^cite_note-Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte_RTVM-CabinetMassOathTaking-418] He appointed his long-time personal aide [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go) as [Special Assistant to the President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Assistant_to_the_President_\(Philippines\)) to provide general supervision to the [Presidential Management Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Management_Staff_\(Philippines\)).[^cite_note-419]

### 2022 Philippine presidential election

Further information: [Ronald dela Rosa and Bong Go 2022 presidential campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_dela_Rosa_and_Bong_Go_2022_presidential_campaigns)



Duterte (left) endorses [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go), who filed his certificate of candidacy for vice president on October 2, 2021.

Initially stating that he "sees nobody deserving" to replace him as the next president,[^cite_note-420] Duterte in August 2021 announced he would run for vice president in the upcoming [2022 national elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_general_election),[^cite_note-421] drawing suspicions from critics that he would extend his term.[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] Duterte's political party, the PDP–Laban Cusi faction, named its [standard bearer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_bearer): former [Philippine National Police](1313055.md) chief and Senator [Ronald dela Rosa](50966529.md),[^cite_note-424] who was widely suspected to be a placeholder for Duterte's daughter, Davao City mayor [Sara](42504861.md).[^cite_note-425] Duterte later withdrew his candidacy, announced his retirement from politics, and had his long-time aide, Senator [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go), substitute him.[^cite_note-426]

Despite polls showing she was the preferred candidate for presidency, Sara decided to settle for the vice presidential race.[^cite_note-Inquirer-ShowOfDismay-427] This prompted dela Rosa to withdraw his candidacy, which Go would later fill in.[^cite_note-428] Sara's move had Duterte dismayed and he initially retracted his planned retirement, announcing he would run for vice president.[^cite_note-Inquirer-ShowOfDismay-427] Ultimately, he decided not to face his daughter, endorsed a Go–Sara tandem, and declared his intent on running as Senator.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] However, Sara decided [to run in tandem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UniTeam) with [Bongbong Marcos](572460.md), who announced his presidential candidacy.[^cite_note-431] Stating his heart and mind contradicted his actions, Go later withdrew his presidential candidacy;[^cite_note-432] shortly after, Duterte officially withdrew his senate bid.[^cite_note-433]

Duterte in November 2021 describes presidential aspirant [Bongbong Marcos](572460.md) as a [cocaine](7701.md) user and "very weak leader".

[^cite_note-434]

Due to his high popularity, Duterte remained influential before the national elections as several presidential candidates were open to his endorsement.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437] As the Cusi faction was left without a standard bearer following Go's withdrawal, allies of Duterte endorsed different candidates. The Cusi faction eventually endorsed presidential candidate Marcos and some officials called for Duterte to do the same.[^cite_note-Inquirer-PDPLabanEndorse-438][^cite_note-439] Stressing he would remain neutral, Duterte refused to heed their calls and insisted on endorsing only Sara as vice president and 17 senatorial candidates.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] Additionally, in an effort to protect their integrity, Duterte barred his Cabinet members from campaigning for any candidate.[^cite_note-MB-CabinetBannedCampaigning-442][^cite_note-443] Despite non-endorsement, Duterte hinted on the next president's preferred qualities: decisive, compassionate, a good judge of a person, and preferably, a lawyer;[^cite_note-Inquirer-PDPLabanEndorse-438] this led a PDP-Laban official to believe Duterte gave a "virtual endorsement" for Vice President [Leni Robredo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leni_Robredo), who was Marcos' chief rival in the presidential race.[^cite_note-444] In March 2022, Go said Duterte briefly met with Marcos and gave him advice on the presidency, but could not say whether Duterte gave Marcos an endorsement.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-Inquirer-PDPLabanEndorse-438]



Duterte (right) witnesses [Sara](42504861.md)'s oath of office as vice president in Davao City on June 19, 2022.

By May 5, 2022, Duterte created [a transition committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_transition_of_Bongbong_Marcos#Duterte's_transition_committee) led by Executive Secretary [Salvador Medialdea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador_Medialdea) to oversee the transition of power to the next administration.[^cite_note-446] Several analysts believe Duterte had his popularity "inherited" by Marcos and Sara, who both won [landslides](41053306.md) in the election.[^cite_note-ABSCBN-DutertePopularity-447]

### Impeachment efforts

Main article: [Efforts to impeach Rodrigo Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efforts_to_impeach_Rodrigo_Duterte)

This section is an excerpt from [Presidency of Rodrigo Duterte § Impeachment attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte#Impeachment_attempt).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte\&action=edit)]

On March 16, 2017, opposition politician Gary Alejano filed an impeachment complaint against Duterte citing thousands of deaths in Duterte's [anti-drug campaign](51032591.md), alleged leadership of vigilante group [Davao Death Squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davao_Death_Squad), and allegations of graft and corruption.[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] Alejano, on March 30, filed a supplemental complaint over Duterte's alleged inaction and "defeatist stance" in the South China Sea, [Scarborough Shoal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scarborough_Shoal), and Benham Rise.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] On May 15, the [House Justice Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Philippine_House_of_Representatives_committees) officially dismissed the charge by unanimous vote due to insufficient evidence after Alejano said he had no personal knowledge of the alleged offenses, having based his impeachment complaint on news reports and witness testimonies.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453]

## Post-presidency (2022–present)

At the end of his presidency, Duterte returned to [Davao City](489401.md) and kept a low profile.[^cite_note-454][^cite_note-PublicusAsia-TopsVoters-455] Former President [Gloria Macapagal-Arroyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloria_Macapagal-Arroyo), a political ally of Duterte, convinced him in September 2023 to participate in the Philippine political landscape.[^cite_note-456] In January 2023, Duterte resumed hosting his weekly show, *[Gikan sa Masa, Para sa Masa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mula_sa_Masa,_Para_sa_Masa)* (From the Masses, For The Masses), on [SMNI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonshine_Media_Network_International); co-hosted by Duterte's friend and spiritual adviser Pastor [Apollo Quiboloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Quiboloy), Duterte would speak about current political issues besetting the country.[^cite_note-SunStar-GikanSaMasa-457] Some of Duterte's strong statements in the show, such as his opposition to the expansion of the [Enhanced Defense Cooperation Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enhanced_Defense_Cooperation_Agreement) (EDCA) sites in the Philippines, attracted attention and were covered by the media.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460]

### 2023 visit to China



Duterte and President Bongbong Marcos meet in [Malacañang Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaca%C3%B1ang_Palace) on August 2, 2023.

In July 2023, amid [escalating tensions in the South China Sea](32005536.md), Chinese President [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) invited Duterte to Beijing. During the meeting, Xi asked Duterte to "play an important role" in improving Philippine-China relations, which were rapidly deteriorating under the Marcos administration that sought to gravitate towards the United States by expanding the number of bases American troops can access under the EDCA.[^cite_note-461] As the meeting did not involve the Marcos administration, the event gained much attention, and the administration wanted details of the Xi-Duterte discussion.[^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463] Duterte visited Malacanang Palace later and, in a closed-door session, discussed the matter with Marcos, though no further details were provided to the media.[^cite_note-464]

### Feud with the House and Marcos-Duterte rift

A rift between the Duterte and Marcos families had begun under [House Speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Speaker_of_the_Philippines) [Martin Romualdez](45505193.md)' leadership.[^cite_note-465] In the House of Representatives, unconfirmed reports of Deputy House Speaker Gloria-Macapagal Arroyo allegedly attempting to unseat Romualdez as House Speaker circulated. Despite Arroyo dismissing the accusations, Arroyo was later demoted by the House from her senior deputy Speaker position.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467]

Duterte's daughter, Vice President and Education Secretary Sara, subsequently resigned from the Romualdez-led [Lakas-CMD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakas-CMD)[^cite_note-468] and, in a veiled reference to Romualdez, called him a "tambaloslos"[^cite_note-469] (A term that can be interpreted as a joke or as an insult, depending on context). In October 2023, Sara requested Congress for the allocation of confidential funds for the [office of the vice president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Vice_President_of_the_Philippines) (OVP) and the [education department](5464548.md) which can be used for the [Reserve Officers' Training Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserve_Officers'_Training_Corps_\(Philippines\)) program. Her requests came under intense scrutiny by the Senate and the House, which was dominated by Romualdez's allies. Congress ultimately rejected her appeals[^cite_note-PhilStar-SaraConfidentialFunds-470] while swiftly approving the confidential funds of the [Office of the President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_President_\(Philippines\));[^cite_note-471] Sara eventually dropped her requests, reasoning that the move was being "divisive".[^cite_note-VPGivesUpFunds-472]

In the aftermath of events, Duterte criticized the House[^cite_note-473] for denying his daughter's confidential funds; alleging a collusion between Romualdez and the left-wing [Makabayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makabayan) bloc,[^cite_note-PhilStar-SaraConfidentialFunds-470] he demanded an [audit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Audit) of the House under Romualdez.[^cite_note-474] Duterte's comments offended some House members[^cite_note-Inquirer-HouseFinds-475] including Romualdez,[^cite_note-476] prompting the House to issue a loyalty check [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resolution_\(law\)) in support of Romualdez; Duterte's political allies, Gloria Macapagal-Arroyo and Congressman [Isidro Ungab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isidro_Ungab), were later expelled by the House from their Deputy House Speaker positions for failing to sign the House resolution.[^cite_note-CNNPH-MoreTectonicShifts-477] Several House members also pledged support for Romualdez,[^cite_note-CNNPH-MoreTectonicShifts-477] including a majority of members of Duterte's political party, [PDP-Laban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PDP-Laban), who [switched parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_switching) mostly to the Romualdez-led [Lakas-CMD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakas-CMD).[^cite_note-478][^cite_note-479] After [ACT Teachers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACT_Teachers_Partylist) Representative [France Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Castro) of the [Makabayan Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makabayan_Bloc) pushed to remove the OVP and education department's confidential funds, Duterte urged Sara to reveal the use of such funds, which included the supposed killing of communists such as Castro.[^cite_note-Inquirer-HouseFinds-475] In response, Castro filed a legal case against Duterte for labeling her as a communist rebel and allegedly threatening her life;[^cite_note-480] Castro's legal case was later denied by the Quezon City Prosecutor's Office due to insufficient evidence.[^cite_note-481]

### SMNI suspension and ICC investigation

Despite sustained domestic popularity,[^cite_note-ManilaTimes-Tops2025SenateBets-482][^cite_note-PublicusAsia-Pahayag-483] Duterte found himself increasingly politically isolated. SMNI, which hosted his talk show *[Gikan sa Masa, Para sa Masa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gikan_sa_Masa,_Para_sa_Masa)*, was later suspended (first for 30 days,[^cite_note-484] and later indefinitely)[^cite_note-485] by the [National Telecommunications Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Telecommunications_Commission) on grounds of alleged dissemination of fake information.[^cite_note-486] Gikan was also suspended after ACT Teachers Representative France Castro filed a complaint against Duterte for allegedly threatening her life during one of the shows.[^cite_note-487] Duterte initially intended to speak to Marcos about the SMNI suspension issue as it has affected his talk show,[^cite_note-488] decrying the suspension as [censorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Censorship).[^cite_note-489] Additionally, the [Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Party_\(Philippines\)), who opposed Marcos' 2022 presidential campaign, were increasingly silent in criticizing the Marcos administration, instead, directing criticism at Duterte and his daughter, Vice President Sara.[^cite_note-490] Duterte and his former aide, Senator [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go), also faced alleged plunder charges initiated in July 2024 by Duterte's staunch critic, former Senator Antonio Trillanes.[^cite_note-491]

Political analysis noted that the Marcos administration subtly shifted its approach toward controversies involving Duterte.[^cite_note-Politiko-IsangSalita-258] Despite Marcos' early assurance of the Philippines' non-cooperation on the looming [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) investigation on Duterte's war on drugs, conflicting statements and moves by House Speaker Romualdez,[^cite_note-492] the House,[^cite_note-493] Marcos' Justice Secretary [Crispin Remulla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crispin_Remulla) and Solicitor General [Menardo Guevarra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menardo_Guevarra) muddled the administration's official stance.[^cite_note-Bolledo-2023-259][^cite_note-494] In addition, Marcos limited his position on the matter through oral statements and avoided issuing an executive order formalizing his stance.[^cite_note-Politiko-IsangSalita-258] There were also unconfirmed reports, including by former Senator Antonio Trillanes, stating that ICC members had already entered the Philippines and were already secretly conducting the investigation.[^cite_note-495] A House probe initiated in April 2024 by Romualdez into a "status quo" agreement in the South China Sea Duterte made during his presidency with Chinese president Xi Jinping was also seen as a "political payback" to Duterte's verbal attacks on Marcos and diminish Duterte's political influence ahead of the 2025 midterm elections.[^cite_note-496] Amidst growing isolation, Duterte found allies who were previously loyal to Marcos. Among them were Marcos' longtime lawyer, [Vic Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vic_Rodriguez_\(lawyer\)), who served as executive secretary in Marcos' first 79 days before resigning;[^cite_note-497] and Marcos' sister, Senator [Imee Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imee_Marcos).[^cite_note-498]

On March 11, 2025, Duterte was arrested upon his arrival at [Ninoy Aquino International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino_International_Airport) by the Filipino police, which enforced an arrest warrant issued by the International Criminal Court.[^cite_note-499]

### Quiboloy's arrest order and Duterte as KOJC administrator

On March 9, 2024, Duterte's longtime friend and [Kingdom of Jesus Christ](47225546.md) leader, Pastor [Apollo Quiboloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Quiboloy), was ordered arrested by the United States [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) after his conviction by a US District Court for alleged cases of sex trafficking, rape, fraud, and cash smuggling.[^cite_note-PhilStar-PropertyAdmin-500] No [extradition](239648.md) request was received by the Marcos administration for Quiboloy's arrest,[^cite_note-501] although a probe in the Senate was previously initiated on January 23 by Senator [Risa Hontiveros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Risa_Hontiveros), who invited Quiboloy to attend the hearings.[^cite_note-502] Quiboloy demanded a fair trial as a requirement to attend,[^cite_note-503] though this was rebuked by Hontiveros and Marcos.[^cite_note-504][^cite_note-505] Claiming his life was under threat through what he described as a connivance between Marcos and the US government,[^cite_note-506] Quiboloy went into hiding and designated Duterte as administrator of his ministry's properties.[^cite_note-PhilStar-PropertyAdmin-500] After Quiboloy refused to attend the hearings, Hontiveros cited him in contempt and recommended the Senate President to order his arrest.[^cite_note-507] Arrest orders for Quiboloy were issued by the Pasig Regional Trial Court despite the efforts of Senators [Robin Padilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_Padilla), [Cynthia Villar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cynthia_Villar), and [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go) to oppose the move.[^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509] Four separate raids were eventually held in Quiboloy's properties in Davao City;[^cite_note-510] though Quiboloy was not found,[^cite_note-511] the raids were criticized by Duterte and his allies for its "excessive \[and] unnecessary force" involving several units of police and military personnel "in full battle gear".[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513][^cite_note-514] KOJC members gathered in a rally in [Liwasang Bonifacio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwasang_Bonifacio) calling for justice for Quiboloy.[^cite_note-515]

### "Hakbang ng Maisug" rallies

Additional nationwide protests, called *[Hakbang ng Maisug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakbang_ng_Maisug)* (brave) prayer rallies, were held in [Dumaguete](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumaguete),[^cite_note-516] [Tagum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tagum),[^cite_note-SunStar-MaisugTagum-517] and [Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angeles,_Pampanga)[^cite_note-518] by Duterte and Quiboloy's supporters, who voiced their grievances against the Marcos administration. Particularly among these grievances included their opposition to [proposed constitutional amendments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_constitutional_reform_attempts_in_the_Philippines)[^cite_note-SunStar-MaisugTagum-517] and to the increased US military presence in the country,[^cite_note-519] as well as denouncement of perceived censorship under the administration.[^cite_note-MB-GovtLimited-520] In one of the rallies, Duterte notably accused Marcos of plotting to extend his term beyond the current term limit of the Constitution and of being a drug addict, to which Marcos responded by alleging Duterte's previous use of [fentanyl](141915.md) as painkiller impaired his judgment;[^cite_note-Rappler-521] Duterte later dared Marcos to undergo a drug test with him at Luneta Park.[^cite_note-Rappler-521]

Frustrated on the administration's use of taxpayers' money,[^cite_note-522] Duterte initially revived calls advocated by former House Speaker [Pantaleon Alvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pantaleon_Alvarez) for [Mindanao](20546.md) to [secede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secede) from the Philippines;[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524] his calls drew disapproval from several lawmakers and former Muslim rebels,[^cite_note-525] prompting Duterte to later retract his calls, saying he only wanted "a better deal for Mindanao".[^cite_note-526] Despite success in holding some rallies, a few suffered setbacks and cancellations after the rally venues were allegedly blocked by the Marcos administration,[^cite_note-527] drawing condemnation from Duterte and his allies.[^cite_note-Inquirer-SlamsDeliberate-528][^cite_note-MB-GovtLimited-520] Despite his criticisms against Marcos, Duterte clarified the prayer rallies were not meant to overthrow the administration[^cite_note-MB-GovtLimited-520] but warned Marcos of extending his six-year term, reminding Marcos of [his father's fate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Power_Revolution).[^cite_note-529]

### 2025 elections

On October 5, 2024, Duterte announced that he will run for [mayor of Davao City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Davao_City), with his son, incumbent Mayor [Sebastian Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Duterte), as his running mate for vice mayor in [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Philippine_general_election). He also rejected calls for him to run for senator, citing his old age.[^cite_note-530] Duterte officially filed his candidacy on October 7;[^cite_note-531] however, on November 7, he welcomed the candidacy of his former cabinet secretary, former [Civil Service Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Service_Commission) chairperson [Karlo Nograles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karlo_Nograles) as Davao City mayor, adding he is retired and wishes to give the position to the next generation.[^cite_note-532]

In May 2025, Duterte was elected as mayor despite his detention at [The Hague](30269.md) by the ICC.[^cite_note-533] However, he was unable to take his oath on the start of his term on June 30, 2025, due to his imprisonment. As a result, his son Sebastian, who was elected vice mayor, became acting mayor.[^cite_note-534] On January 23, 2026, Sebastian was sworn in as mayor of Davao City, following his father's failure to take the oath of office within the six-month prescribed period due to his detention.[^cite_note-535]

### House and Senate probe into the war on drugs

Duterte takes "full, legal responsibility" for the actions of the police who he said merely followed his orders, during the [Senate Blue Ribbon Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Blue_Ribbon_Committee) hearing on October 28, 2024.

[^cite_note-536]

Duterte during the House probe on the war on drugs on November 13, 2024

In October 2024, the House quad committee led an investigation into the supposed use of intelligence funds to fund an alleged "reward system" for police involved in drug-related extrajudicial killings that occurred during Duterte's presidency. Among the witnesses in the House probe was former [Philippine Charity Sweepstakes Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippine_Charity_Sweepstakes_Office) general manager and retired Police Colonel [Royina Garma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royina_Garma), who accused Duterte of wanting to apply his Davao model approach of the war on drugs on a national scale. Senators [Ronald dela Rosa](50966529.md) and [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go), dismissing the reward money claims as baseless accusations and hearsay, launched a parallel probe in the Senate to "find out the truth".[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538] Responding to the Senate invitation, Duterte attended the Senate probe into his campaign against illegal drugs. During the session, Duterte admitted to using a "death squad" to combat crime as Davao mayor, but denied directing extrajudicial killings in his national drug war, now being examined by the ICC.[^cite_note-WashingtonTimes-2024-539] He further noted the resumption of activities of drug dealers and criminals after he stepped down from presidency, telling them "If I'm given another chance, I'll wipe all of you".[^cite_note-WashingtonTimes-2024-539]

> My mandate as president of the republic was to protect the country and the Filipino people. Do not question my policies because I offer no apologies, no excuses. I did what I had to do.

— Duterte, October 28, 2024[^cite_note-540]

Duterte accepted an invitation to the House quad committee hearing on November 13. During the hearing, he reiterated the tough but necessary decisions he took in his anti-drug campaign. He further said he was not scared of the ICC and expressed impatience for the ICC's investigation, saying "I am already old, I might die soon. You might miss the pleasure of seeing me standing before the court hearing the judgement whatever it is". In a significant reversal of position, Marcos' executive secretary [Lucas Bersamin](22247470.md) later stated the Marcos administration "will feel obliged to consider" a [red notice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_notice) from the [Interpol](14338129.md) for Duterte should it be issued.[^cite_note-541]

### Impeachment of Sara Duterte

In December 2024, Duterte offered to join the defense team of his daughter, Vice President [Sara Duterte](42504861.md), against [her impeachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_impeachment_of_Sara_Duterte).[^cite_note-542] On February 18, 2025, two weeks after the vice president was impeached by the [House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Representatives_\(Philippines\)), Duterte was listed as the legal counsel of his daughter in her [Supreme Court](1335534.md) petition to stop the impeachment from proceeding to trial.[^cite_note-543]

### Arrest and detention at the International Criminal Court

Main article: [Arrest of Rodrigo Duterte](79430656.md)



Following his arrest, Duterte was accompanied by [Salvador Medialdea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador_Medialdea) on the flight to The Hague into ICC custody

On March 7, 2025, Duterte left for [Hong Kong](13404.md) alongside [Honeylet Avanceña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honeylet_Avance%C3%B1a), his daughter [Veronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitty_Duterte) and his entourage.[^cite_note-544] Although his lawyer [Salvador Panelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvador_Panelo) said that his purpose of travel was to attend a [KOJC](47225546.md)-organized campaign sortie for [Partido Demokratiko Pilipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Demokratiko_Pilipino) among [overseas Filipino workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Filipino_workers), his departure coincided with reports that the release of an arrest warrant for Duterte from the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) was imminent.[^cite_note-545] Others who attended the event include Duterte's daughter, Vice President Sara Duterte, Senators [Ronald dela Rosa](50966529.md) and [Bong Go](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bong_Go), and senatorial candidates [Rodante Marcoleta](68240498.md) and [Vic Rodriguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vic_Rodriguez_\(lawyer\)).[^cite_note-546]

On March 11, 2025, upon his return from Hong Kong on a commercial flight, Duterte was arrested at [Ninoy Aquino International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninoy_Aquino_International_Airport) for [crimes against humanity](61099.md) of murder, torture, and rape.[^cite_note-547][^cite_note-548] The warrant was issued by the ICC, but coursed through [Interpol](14338129.md). A top official of the Marcos administration earlier said that the government would honor its commitments to Interpol if an arrest warrant was issued, despite its policy of not cooperating with the ICC.[^cite_note-549] Duterte questioned the warrant's legitimacy, asserting that the arrest did not follow standard procedures and that any prosecution should occur within the Philippines. His supporters rallied in major cities condemning the arrest, while activists hailed the arrest as a pivotal moment for justice.[^cite_note-550] He was soon surrendered to the ICC in [The Hague](30269.md), Netherlands, where he was being held as of 11 March 2025.[^cite_note-2025-03-11_NPR-551]

The indictment against Duterte, which was made public on September 22, 2025, accused him of involvement in the murders of 76 people between 2013 and 2018. Fifty-seven of the killings took place during his presidency, and the other 19 occurred during his third term as mayor of Davao City.[^cite_note-552] On October 10, 2025, the ICC rejected a request from Duterte to be released from detention, arguing that he posed a risk of fleeing and would not be willing to return for trial, as well as presenting concerns about potential witness intimidation if he were granted freedom.[^cite_note-553] On January 26, 2026, the ICC declared Duterte fit for trial, overruling efforts by his lawyers to have the charges dismissed on health grounds.[^cite_note-554] Duterte waived his right to appear at any proceedings, with his lawyer [Nicholas Kaufman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Kaufman) stating that he was not mentally fit to follow the hearing.[^cite_note-555] On April 23, 2026, the Pre-Trial Chamber of the ICC [committed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committal_procedure) Duterte to trial.[^cite_note-556][^cite_note-557]

In May 2026, the ICC Pre-Trial Chamber I denied Duterte's appeal to dismiss the charges.[^cite_note-558] That same month the Trial Chamber III of the ICC set the opening statements in Duterte's trial to begin on November 30, 2026.[^cite_note-559] On September 3, 2026, his defense asked the ICC to verify [digital evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_evidence) presented by the prosecution, saying that much of the material against Duterte had been taken from [open-source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-source) and [user-generated content](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/User-generated_content) from the internet.[^cite_note-560]

## Public image



Duterte meets with the [Filipino community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipinos_in_Indonesia) in [Jakarta](16275.md), September 9, 2016.

Ardent supporters of Duterte have been labeled as "[Diehard Duterte Supporters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diehard_Duterte_Supporters)", alternatively known as "Digong Duterte Supporters", which shares the acronym with the [Davao Death Squad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davao_Death_Squad) (DDS).[^cite_note-561] This label has been applied to the 16 million people who voted for him in the 2016 presidential election.[^cite_note-562]

Duterte developed a reputation as a "protector" and "savior" in his hometown of [Davao City](489401.md) as mayor of the city for more than two decades. This is despite reports of death squads in the city.[^cite_note-vox-563]



Duterte with US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in [Pasay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasay), Metro Manila, November 2017

Duterte has been described as a [populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populist), with his foul-mouthed remarks against the country's elite which positioned him as a "man of the people" and was critical to his victory in the 2016 presidential election.[^cite_note-564] He has also been compared to [U.S. President](24113.md) [Donald Trump](4848272.md) for his rhetorical style.[^cite_note-vox-563]

Duterte and his administration have been criticized for his anti-drug campaign, foreign policies, human rights record, extrajudicial killings during his term, political views, and controversial comments.[^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566] Despite the criticisms on his administration, throughout his career, Duterte's remained hugely popular, attributed to his man-of-the-people style and a perception of strong leadership and success in fighting crime and corruption, while opponents reproach him for his authoritarian style and low tolerance of dissent.[^cite_note-567] Analysts attribute his continued popularity to his emotional connection to the public, citing his charisma and humor, tough-talking manner, his image as a father figure as *Tatay Digong* (Father Digong), and Filipinos' general interest in strong leaders.[^cite_note-Rappler-TatayDigong-568][^cite_note-BusinessWorld-TatayDigong-569]

A [Social Weather Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Weather_Stations) study concluded that there are multiple reasons for Duterte's high satisfaction ratings in surveys; these include his strong [base support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_base), satisfaction with the administration's overall governance (*pamamalakad*) and with some policy issues which include helping the poor and the drug war, and his character. Poll respondents who relate to or are attracted to some aspects of his character, such as his perceived decisiveness and diligence tend to be satisfied. On the other hand, those who feel he is vulgar (*bastos*) tend to be less satisfied.[^cite_note-SWS-DuterteSpecialReport-570]

Despite keeping a low profile during his post-presidency, Duterte retained his popularity, topping four consecutive surveys conducted separately in March,[^cite_note-PublicusAsia-TopsVoters-455] June,[^cite_note-ManilaTimes-Tops2025SenateBets-482] September,[^cite_note-571] and December 2023 by PUBLiCUS Asia on the voting disposition for 2025 Philippine Senate election.[^cite_note-PublicusAsia-Pahayag-483]

### Controversial remarks and human rights concerns

See also: [Protests against Rodrigo Duterte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Rodrigo_Duterte)

In 2015, Duterte made homophobic remarks about United States ambassador Philipp Goldberg after Secretary of State John Kerry visited the Philippines.[^cite_note-572]

Duterte's record on human rights and his long history of comments that have widely been considered to be offensive, provocative, threatening, and undiplomatic have received sharp international criticism. He has been portrayed by his critics in the media as having a "dirty mouth".[^cite_note-573] He had, however, promised to behave in a "prim and proper" manner on the national and international stage once he was to be inaugurated as president, to the point that, "almost, I would become holy."[^cite_note-574]

Throughout his presidency, Duterte has made controversial comments about rape,[^cite_note-575][^cite_note-576][^cite_note-577] human rights, his views on media killings,[^cite_note-Reuters-Denounced-578] and has used slurs;[^cite_note-Croucher-2019-579][^cite_note-580] he has also made controversial statements to international leaders and institutions.[^cite_note-XinhuaNet-581][^cite_note-582] He has also repeatedly criticized the [Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_the_Philippines) which has expressed alarm over deaths linked to the war on drugs.[^cite_note-583][^cite_note-anticleric-584][^cite_note-church-585][^cite_note-586]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW) called the first year of Duterte in office a "human rights calamity". HRW estimates that there have been 7,000 deaths from the day Duterte first took office to January 2017.[^cite_note-587] The Duterte administration suspended the drug war in February 2017 in an effort to cleanse the police ranks of supposed corruption, also halting the disclosure of figures on deaths related to drug arrests and raids.[^cite_note-588] In March 2017, HRW released a special investigation and report on the state of police related shooting, titled "License To Kill".[^cite_note-589] *The New York Times* had also released a video documentary "When A President Says I'll Kill You", which depicts Duterte's war on drugs through a local photographer's eyes.[^cite_note-590] On August 17, 2017, HRW called Duterte a threat to the human rights community after he made threats against human rights activists.[^cite_note-591]

### Supporters

Main article: [Diehard Duterte Supporters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diehard_Duterte_Supporters)

See also: [Mocha Uson Blog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mocha_Uson_Blog) and [Thinking Pinoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thinking_Pinoy)

Several other Facebook groups with the acronym "DDS" supported Duterte as early as 2011. Among these groups is the Duterte Defense Squad, which was created on July 5, 2011. Other examples include Digong Duterte Supporters-Registered Nurses Group, Duterte's Destiny is to Serve the Country, Digong Duterte Swerte (lit. 'Digong Duterte is (Good) Luck'), and Davsur Duterte Supporters. In 2015, members of these groups urged Duterte to run for president.[^cite_note-592]

### Approval ratings

Main article: [Opinion polling on the Rodrigo Duterte presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Rodrigo_Duterte_presidency)

| Date[^cite_note-593] | Rating |
| --- | --- |
| Sep 2016 | +64 |
| Dec 2016 | +63 |
| Mar 2017 | +63 |
| Jun 2017 | +66 |
| Sep 2017 | +48 |
| Dec 2017 | +58 |
| Mar 2018 | +56 |
| Jun 2018 | +45 |
| Sep 2018 | +54 |
| Dec 2018 | +60 |
| Mar 2019 | +66 |
| Jun 2019 | +68 |
| Sep 2019 | +65 |
| Dec 2019 | +72 |
| Nov 2020 | +79 |
| May 2021 | +65 |
| Jun 2021 | +62 |
| Sep 2021 | +52 |
| Dec 2021[^cite_note-MB-SWS4thQtr-594] | +60 |
| Apr 2022[^cite_note-595] | +65 |
| Jun 2022[^cite_note-SWS-2ndQ-596] | +81 |

Despite criticism and international opposition to his controversial anti-narcotics drive, Duterte's domestic approval rating remained relatively high throughout his presidency.[^cite_note-ABCNews-WeedonAlan-39] Duterte began his presidency with a high trust rating of 79% and 91% (the highest of any Philippine official since 1999), from pollsters [Social Weather Stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Weather_Stations) (SWS) and [Pulse Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulse_Asia), respectively.[^cite_note-597][^cite_note-598] His net satisfaction rating reached its lowest at 45% in July 2018, attributed to inflation during the period as well as Duterte calling God "stupid" for the concept of [original sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_sin) in the [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible);[^cite_note-Nicolas-2018-599] eventually, his satisfaction rating rose to 60% by the end of the year.[^cite_note-600]

Halfway through his term, Duterte's approval rating reached 79%, the highest among his predecessors at this stage in their presidencies.[^cite_note-601] It rose to 87% by the end of 2019 due to the successful hosting of the [2019 Southeast Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Southeast_Asian_Games) as well as the administration's poverty reduction programs.[^cite_note-602] Amid the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Philippines), Pulse Asia reported in September 2020 that 84% of Filipinos approved of the administration's efforts to contain the virus and provide assistance to those who lost their jobs; 92% of the poll respondents also agreed Duterte had "done well" in preventing the contagion in the country.[^cite_note-PNAGovPH-COVIDSurvey-603][^cite_note-ASEANPost-604] Duterte ended 2021 with an approval rating of 60%, wherein higher net satisfaction was observed among those who have been or are willing to get vaccinated.[^cite_note-MB-SWS4thQtr-594]

Duterte maintained his high approval and trust ratings as he approached the end of his term.[^cite_note-605][^cite_note-Philippine_News_Agency-40] A 2021 poll by WR Numero Research revealed that 54.59% of voters want soft continuity of Duterte's policies, 29.57% want full continuity, while only 15.84% preferred change.[^cite_note-606] Duterte left office with a net satisfaction rating of 81%, his highest in SWS polling.[^cite_note-SWS-2ndQ-596] Additionally, polling firm PUBLiCUS Asia reported in Duterte's last month in office that Duterte received a 75% approval for his entire six-year tenure, making him the most popular post-[EDSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EDSA_Revolution) president.[^cite_note-607][^cite_note-Cruz-2022-41]

Duterte described himself as [left-leaning](18499.md) during his campaign and presidency, but has stressed that he is for democracy and is not a communist.[^cite_note-608][^cite_note-palatino2017-609] Some in the mainstream news media have labeled Duterte as a right-wing populist and authoritarian.[^cite_note-610][^cite_note-611][^cite_note-docena\&hetland2016-612][^cite_note-613][^cite_note-614] He has been likened to other nationalist figures in the 2010s including [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md).[^cite_note-615]

Duterte was a member of the leftist [Kabataang Makabayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabataang_Makabayan) during the 1970s[^cite_note-616] and was a student of prominent Philippine leftist figure and founder of the [Communist Party of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Philippines), [Jose Maria Sison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jose_Maria_Sison).[^cite_note-617][^cite_note-618] During a 1980s interview, Duterte expressed his frustration on seeing suspected criminals being acquitted despite evidence against them, further adding he admired the NPA's "brand of rendering swift justice".[^cite_note-Gonzales-2005-79]

His relationship with the communists deteriorated during his presidency due to continued rebel attacks on soldiers despite the peace talks.[^cite_note-CNNPH-DuterteFires-306][^cite_note-Inquirer-LastLeft-307] The collapse of the peace talks with the CPP, [New People's Army](1262096.md) (NPA) and [NDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Front_of_the_Philippines) led to the falling-out between Duterte and the CPP. The dismissal of cabinet members, most of whom were dropped by the [Commission on Appointments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_on_Appointments) or by Duterte himself, led to his erstwhile progressive allies disowning him. The fallout between Duterte and the CPP has also been followed by the expansion of a [historical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Scare) and [religiously informed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decree_against_Communism) cultural hostility toward left-wing politics in the Philippines, which had been previously reserved for the CPP but which has now spread to the social democratic, democratic socialist and national democratic organizations, such as the [Makabayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makabayan) bloc.[^cite_note-palatino2017-609][^cite_note-lalu2021-619][^cite_note-contreras2020a-620] Some national democrats and others who identify with the left have refuted Duterte's self-proclaimed socialist credentials given his inability to concretely and seriously tackle the economic aspects of liberalism.[^cite_note-palatino2017-609][^cite_note-purugganan\&al2017-621][^cite_note-docena2017-622][^cite_note-bello2018-623][^cite_note-chúa2020-624][^cite_note-magsalin2020-625]

## Personal life

Duterte is known for being an avid fan of large-displacement [cruiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruiser_\(motorcycle\)) motorcycles,[^cite_note-628] but detests luxury cars. He owns a [Harley-Davidson Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harley-Davidson_Street)[^cite_note-hdstreet-629] and a [Can-Am Spyder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Can-Am_Spyder).[^cite_note-630] He was once a habitual [smoker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smoking), but he eventually quit after a doctor's suggestion due to health concerns. Duterte is an avid reader of [Robert Ludlum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Ludlum) and [Sidney Sheldon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidney_Sheldon) novels.[^cite_note-Duterte22-631] Duterte is also known for his straightforward and vocal attitude in public, especially in interviews, showing no hesitation in profusely using [profanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Profanity) live on-screen on numerous occasions despite formal requests by media groups and schools beforehand to abstain.[^cite_note-632]

Duterte has his own local show in Davao City called *[Gikan sa Masa, para sa Masa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gikan_sa_Masa,_para_sa_Masa)* ("From the Masses, For the Masses"), which formerly aired as a [blocktimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Block_programming) on [ABS-CBN Davao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DXAS-TV), and currently broadcast on [SMNI News Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SMNI_News_Channel) since 2023.[^cite_note-633] He is also a member of [Lex Talionis Fraternitas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lex_Talionis_Fraternitas), a fraternity based in the [San Beda College of Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Beda_College_of_Law) and the [Ateneo de Davao University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ateneo_de_Davao_University).[^cite_note-634]

Aside from his native [Cebuano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cebuano_language), Duterte is also fluent in [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_language) and [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language).[^cite_note-635]

While criticizing political opponent [Antonio Trillanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Trillanes) in a 2019 speech, Duterte claimed that he was once gay but had ["cured himself"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ex-gay_movement) before meeting his wife [Elizabeth Zimmerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Zimmerman).[^cite_note-Croucher-2019-579]

Since being the mayor of Davao City, Duterte has held an annual tradition of visiting children with cancer in the city and giving them Christmas gifts.[^cite_note-636]

Duterte is a self-proclaimed womanizer who had other women besides his former wife [Elizabeth Zimmerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Zimmerman) and current domestic partner [Honeylet Avanceña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honeylet_Avance%C3%B1a). According to his son [Paolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Duterte), the elder Duterte had 13 girlfriends based in Davao City. Sebastian only claims two other women besides Zimmerman and Avanceña.[^cite_note-637][^cite_note-638]

### Family



Duterte (seated, left) with his first family after delivering his third State of the Nation Address in 2018



Duterte with Avanceña along with their daughter Veronica, about to be on their way to [Malacañan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaca%C3%B1an) for his [inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Rodrigo_Duterte) and start of his [presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Rodrigo_Duterte)&#x20;

Duterte's father, Vicente, died in 1968 while his mother, Soledad, died on February 4, 2012, at the age of 95.[^cite_note-639]

In 1973, Duterte married [Elizabeth Zimmerman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Zimmerman), a flight attendant of [German American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_American) descent[^cite_note-CNNPH-ZimmermanDigong-640][^cite_note-641] who traces her roots in [Tuburan, Cebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuburan,_Cebu).[^cite_note-642] They have three children (from eldest to youngest): [Paolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Duterte) ("Pulong"), [Sara](42504861.md) ("Inday Sara") and [Sebastian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Duterte) ("Baste").[^cite_note-CNNPH-ZimmermanDigong-640] In the 1990s, his son Paolo left the family for more than five years after having a son ([Omar Vincent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omar_Duterte)) with Lovelie Sangkola in 1994, which Duterte acknowledged to be "\[o]ne of my sad events in my life".[^cite_note-643] Sara and Paolo both entered politics in 2007 and 2008 respectively, while Baste, with no interest in politics, initially concentrated on business and surfing but eventually ran and won as vice mayor of Davao City in 2019.[^cite_note-644][^cite_note-645] Sara was elected as [Vice President](2271639.md) in [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Philippine_presidential_election).[^cite_note-ABSCBN-DutertePopularity-447]

Duterte has been publicly open about his infidelity while married to Zimmerman and cited it as the reason for his failed marriage. According to newspaper columnist [Ramon Tulfo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramon_Tulfo), Duterte's various alleged partners included "a then [agriculture undersecretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernadette_Romulo-Puyat), a [governor of a province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelica_Amante), a [local TV newscaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girlie_Balaba) and a policewoman",[^cite_note-646] with Duterte himself admitting in 2016 to having multiple girlfriends such as a cosmetics worker in a shopping mall and a cashier.[^cite_note-647] In 1998, Zimmerman filed a petition in the [Regional Trial Court](36842278.md) of [Pasig](359702.md) to nullify her marriage. Duterte never appeared in court and did not contest Zimmerman's petition. Two years later, the court decided in her favor, ending the 27-year marriage of Duterte and Zimmerman.[^cite_note-Hofilena-2015-648] Duterte and Zimmerman have been on good terms in recent years,[^cite_note-Zimmerman-649] with Zimmerman stating: "Yes, \[Rodrigo] is really a very good leader. That is all he is. But when it comes to family, he is not capable of taking care of it."[^cite_note-Hofilena-2015-648] His daughter, Sara, characterized her family as "broken", calling her relationship with her father as "not very close" up to the time she became mayor in 2010.[^cite_note-MayorsDuterte-650][^cite_note-spotlight-651][^cite_note-eyes-652]

Prior to his arrest, Duterte had been living with his [common-law wife](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common-law_marriage) [Cielito "Honeylet" Avanceña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honeylet_Avance%C3%B1a), a businesswoman and former nurse, with whom he has one daughter named [Veronica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kitty_Duterte) ("Kitty"), an American by birth.[^cite_note-653] Duterte has eleven grandchildren, half of whom are Muslims and the other half Christian,[^cite_note-BoholNewsToday-Conflict-654] and two great-grandchildren.[^cite_note-655] Since 2022, a child named Mira ("Mimi", born 2019) is seen being raised by Duterte and Avanceña alongside Veronica, though there are conflicting reports on whether she is a child or a grandchild of Duterte.[^cite_note-656][^cite_note-657][^cite_note-658][^cite_note-659][^cite_note-660] Veronica attributes her rose tattoo to Mira.[^cite_note-661][^cite_note-662]

On his paternal side, Duterte shares familial ties with some of the prominent families of the Visayas, particularly the Almendrases and Duranos of [Danao, Cebu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danao,_Cebu).[^cite_note-duterte-durano-almendras-664] His distant uncle, former Senator [Alejandro Almendras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Almendras), was one of his original supporters when he first ran for mayor of Davao City in [1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1988_Davao_City_elections).

### Religion



Duterte (center) and his cabinet members pray before the start of the 6th Cabinet Meeting on September 14, 2016.

Despite being raised as a communicant of the [Catholic Church](606848.md), on January 19, 2016, Duterte clarified that he had not attended [Mass](5684685.md) for quite some time already since he deemed it incompatible with his mayoral responsibilities: "If I listened to the [Ten Commandments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ten_Commandments) or to the priests," said Duterte, "I would not be able to do anything as a mayor."[^cite_note-665] Duterte has described himself as [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian) in 2016[^cite_note-Christian-666][^cite_note-Torres-2016-667] and "[spiritual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spiritual_but_not_religious)" in 2018, expressing that he "can't accept" Catholicism or [organized religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organized_religion) but also that he is neither an [atheist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) nor an [agnostic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnosticism).[^cite_note-PNAGovPH-Agnostic-668][^cite_note-Newshub-2018-669] He has referred to God as [Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allah)[^cite_note-Torres-2016-667] and has been quoted as saying: "a part of me which is [Islam](6037917.md)".[^cite_note-670]

During his presidency, Duterte and the [Catholic Church in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_the_Philippines) exchanged frequent criticisms. In response to the rising death toll in Duterte's war on drugs, the [Catholic Bishops' Conference of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Bishops'_Conference_of_the_Philippines) (CBCP) in February 2017 issued a strongly worded [pastoral letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastoral_letter) which was read in church services; the letter highlighted that "\[killing] is considered as normal", and "to consent and keep silent in front of evil is to be an accomplice to it".[^cite_note-Patag-2018-671] Duterte later lambasted Catholic bishops, telling them "not use the [pulpit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulpit)", reviling them as "sons of bitches", remarking that some of them are gay, and urging street bums to rob and kill them.[^cite_note-Aurelio-Corrales-2019-672] Notable among the bishops Duterte criticized was Caloocan Bishop [Pablo Virgilio David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Virgilio_David), a vocal critic of the Duterte administration's war on drugs. Duterte accused David of being involved in illegal drugs and stealing church donations,[^cite_note-Patag-2018-671] after which the CBCP came to David's defense and vouched for his integrity.[^cite_note-673] Citing continued criticism from Catholic bishops, Duterte's verbal attacks on the Catholic Church escalated in mid-2018 to early 2019,[^cite_note-Patag-2018-671][^cite_note-Aurelio-Corrales-2019-672] during which he challenged the Catholic Church to show evidence of the existence of God, while expressing his belief in "one Supreme God" and "a universal mind somewhere which controls the universe".[^cite_note-PNAGovPH-Agnostic-668][^cite_note-Newshub-2018-669] In one of his harshest vitriol in June 2018, he remarked that God was "stupid" for permitting [the serpent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serpents_in_the_Bible) to tempt [Eve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eve) in [the creation story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genesis_creation_narrative) in the [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible); though the controversial statement was later clarified by his spokesperson as Duterte's "personal opinion",[^cite_note-AlJazeera-GodRemark-674] it contributed to Duterte's quarterly approval rating dipping to its lowest at 45%.[^cite_note-Nicolas-2018-599] Efforts by the Church to rally public support against the administration's war on drugs, however, were less effective due to Duterte's domestic popularity and high trust ratings.[^cite_note-675]



Duterte guests in [Apollo Quiboloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Quiboloy)'s (center) [SMNI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonshine_Media_Network_International) show, *Give Us This Day*, in 2019.

Duterte considers his close friend, pastor [Apollo Quiboloy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_Quiboloy) of the [Kingdom of Jesus Christ](47225546.md) (KJC), as his spiritual adviser.[^cite_note-676][^cite_note-677] Duterte would become estate administrator of the properties KJC in March 2024, following Senate inquiries launched against the church and Quiboloy.[^cite_note-PhilStar-PropertyAdmin-500]

In his post-presidency in January 2024, Duterte visited the wake of CBCP president Fernando Capalla in [Davao Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davao_Cathedral), during which he stated he attends Masses thrice every Sunday; he further added "I am a very holy man. I'm a whole human being now".[^cite_note-678]

### Health



Rodrigo Duterte with Laotian President [Bounnhang Vorachith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bounnhang_Vorachith) in [Vientiane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vientiane), Laos on September 7, 2016

Duterte has [Buerger's disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thromboangiitis_obliterans), an inflammation of blood vessels, mostly in the limbs, and [Barrett's esophagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrett's_esophagus), wherein esophageal cells are gradually replaced by gastrointestinal cells. He has denied rumors of [throat cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Throat_cancer).[^cite_note-679]

Duterte admitted in December 2016 that he had been a user of the addictive [opioid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opioid) drug [Fentanyl](141915.md). He said that a doctor prescribed the drug to alleviate back pain and headaches, but that he would take more than he was prescribed.[^cite_note-680] Duterte later denied that he was a drug addict, and a spokesman stated that he was not affected by side-effects of the drug, which include confusion, anxiety, and [hallucinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallucination).[^cite_note-681]

Duterte has boasted about his use of [Viagra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viagra): "When I was young, I could do overnight, which is more expensive. When I got old, I could do short time only because I have such a short time left. After one erection, that's it. No more. Without Viagra, it's even more difficult."[^cite_note-682][^cite_note-683]

A psychological assessment of Duterte was commissioned by a doctor named Natividad Dayan during Duterte's marriage annulment to Elizabeth Zimmerman in July 1998. The result was that Duterte (then Davao City mayor) was found to have "[antisocial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisocial_personality_disorder) [narcissistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narcissistic_personality_disorder) personality disorder", exemplified by "gross indifference, insensitivity and self-centeredness", and a "grandiose sense of self-entitlement and manipulative behaviors". According to the assessment, he had a "pervasive tendency to demean, humiliate others and violate their rights and feelings", and was "unable to reflect on the consequences of his actions."[^cite_note-684]

In a speech to the Filipino community in [Russia](25391.md), Duterte revealed that he has [myasthenia gravis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myasthenia_gravis), a neuromuscular disease, which makes his eye droop.[^cite_note-685][^cite_note-686]

## Honors and awards

### Honorary doctorates

Duterte declined an honorary doctor of laws degree offered by the [University of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_the_Philippines) amid opposition from members of the university.[^cite_note-687] Duterte was conferred an [honorary doctorate degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_degree) for [international relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_relations) or foreign diplomacy[^cite_note-688] from the [Moscow State Institute of International Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moscow_State_Institute_of_International_Relations) on October 5, 2019.[^cite_note-689] Duterte received an honorary [Juris Doctor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juris_Doctor) degree from his alma mater, San Beda College of Law in November 2023.[^cite_note-690]

### Duterte Day

On March 28, 2023, the Davao City Council passed a resolution declaring every March 28—Duterte's birthday—as "Duterte Day" in recognition for the "great and continuing contribution" of Duterte to the city and the Philippines.[^cite_note-691]

### IBP Golden Pillar of Law Award

Duterte was conferred the Golden Pillar of Law Award on September 15, 2025, from the Davao City chapter of the [Integrated Bar of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_Bar_of_the_Philippines) (IBP).[^cite_note-692][^cite_note-pillarreview-693] Human rights lawyers and activists urged the IBP to revoke the award viewing that his role in the war on drugs contradicts with the spirit of the recognition.[^cite_note-IBPurged-694][^cite_note-nupl-goldenpillar-695]

The national office of the IBP maintained Duterte's [presumed innocence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presumed_innocence) as it "do not confer guilt or grace beyond the reach of law" but nevertheless pledge to review rules concerning the award.[^cite_note-pillarreview-693][^cite_note-IBPurged-694] IBP Davao insist that Duterte satisfy the criteria for the award based on professional longevity and standing status and that the Golden Pillar award does not "serve as an endorsement of political acts or personal ideologies".[^cite_note-nupl-goldenpillar-695]
